Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly The production of sunflowers and sunflower oil is strategic for our country's economy and community health. The most fat sunflowers in our country are produced in the districts of Thrace-Marmara (47.2), Tekirdağ (17.8 per cent), Edirne (14.9 per cent), 40lari (11.4%) and Adana (6.6 per cent), Çum (3.9 per cent), Tokat (3.4%), Aksaray (2.9 per cent), Amas (2.9%) and Amasian (2.3%). The Marmara-Tracian region is followed by the Central Anatolian region with 29.2 per cent. The remaining 12 per cent of our country's sunflower production is conducted in the Black Sea, 8.7 per cent in the Mediterranean and 2.8% in Eastern and Southeast Anatolian regions. Turkey is one of the largest use of sunflower oil in the world. In our country, sunflower oil consumption is approximately 900,000 tonnes annually. However, local sunflower oil is around 400-450,000 tonnes annually. It's covered with open imports. Thus, sunflower is one of the major imports of our country, and Turkey is the largest importer country since the European Union. In 2013, 710,843 tonnes of seeds were imported, 625,849 tons of sunflower oil, 556,909 tons of seeds and 812,401 tons of sunflower oil were imported. Given the climate and soil properties of our country, although it has great potential for the production of fatty seed plants, it is unable to produce enough oil to meet our needs. Turkey makes about half of its oiled sunflower seed imports from Bulgaria, and most of the other part from Ukraine, Romania, Russia and Moldova, and about half of the sunflower oil imports from Ukraine, and most of the other part from Russia, Argentina, Romania and Bulgaria. Bulgaria receives about 50 per cent of seed imports, while Ukraine receives about 50 per cent of raw oil imports. Turkey's sunflower potential production area is 1,450,000 ha. However, this potential production area is being used for about 650,000 ha (e.g. The 800,000 habitats are planted, and on average, if the yield is 170 kilograms per year, the sunflower will be worth 1,450,000 tonnes of production per year, so that the plantary oil deficit of our country will be met with imports. The absence of a stable plan for sunflower production leads to insufficient use of the existing production potential, resulting in increased sunflower oil deficits, and the phase of industry's dependence on the outside. The lack of competition with external market prices due to high production costs in fatty seeds, the failure to compete with alternative products in areas where they are raised due to low income in the unit area, the low price of the world's crude oils compared to Turkey, the failure of policies to plan products and increase the production of fatty seeds, are listed as reasons for the inefficient production of oil seeds in Turkey. There hasn't been a solution that could cover this production deficit for years. This makes our herbal oil industry dependent on import vulnerable to fluctuations in the world market. For all these reasons, I respectfully submit what must be done to improve domestic production instead of importing sunflowers, and to determine the problems of the sunflower maker, the opening of Parliamental research under Articles 98, Article 104 and 105 of the Constitution. The first iron steel plant was built in the 1930s in Kırıkkale. In 1975, Alexanderun Iron Steel Factory, another integrated facility, began production to respond to the development of the iron steel demand. Turkey is among the world's leading producers in the iron steel industry. In 2014, our country produced 2 percent of global steel production of 34 million tons. At this level of production, it is ranked eighth in the world and second in Europe after Germany. In Turkey, which became one of the world's leading raw steel producers, with investments accelerating in the 2000s, the sector's production capacity increased by 150 per cent from 2000-2013 to 50 million tonnes/years. The companies operating in the sector are clustered in four regions. In recent times, investments are concentrated both in Alexanderun and around Osmaniye because of its position near the Middle East, from key steel production centers. The sector is among Turkey's leading export sectors. The iron steel industry is the third most exported sector after motor vehicles and ready clothing industry. The exports of the iron steel sector, which single-handedly carried out 10% of total exports, took place at $15.2 billion, with a reduction of 5 per cent in the amount compared to 2013, and at a cost of 4 per cent decline. Turkey is dependent on imports in terms of raw materials. The problem with supply markets is directly reflected in the sector. Turkey's producers depend on production structure and raw imports, compared to their international competitors, having a cost-effective disadvantage, bringing market losses. Furthermore, in parallel with concentrated global competition, countries are increasing their measures to protect their sectors. The additional tax that the United States, Canada, Australia, Jordan, Morocco, Thailand and Egypt bring to specific Turkish products is one of the examples. It is estimated that market losses will increase in this framework in the coming period. It is said that 40 per cent of our country's iron steel production took place, that Hatay, where 18,000 workers worked in its facilities, deepened the crisis of the state's pressure on companies in Alexanderun and the Fourways districts, and that many companies are having difficulty competing with the world and are also considering leaving the sector. In September, Turkey's total production of raw steel dropped at an extraordinary rate of 14.1% compared to last year's same month, while raw steel production in electric arcs dropped at 23.4%. Turkey's raw steel production in September reflects one of the worst performances in recent years. The sector's input costs are needed primarily to be put on the table, to end any funding and cuts that lower competitiveness, to reduce energy input costs and to be immediately taken to prevent dumpping imports. Some companies in Turkey's top 500 industrial companies, including the top 100, who have built mosques, schools, bridges and signed many social projects in the region, stopping production and the beginning of layoffs will cause serious problems in the region. The sale of 18 per cent of half of the product in the iron steel industry with zero VAT is one of the few problems of the industry being governed by legislation thirty years ago. There must be a solution to the problems of fire shares, VAT rates, energy costs and the sector. The area of Fourways, Payas, and Alexanderun will face great tribulations in the event of the spread of layoffs and the halting of production that concerns 18,000 families working in Alexander's territory. For reasons stated above, parliamentary research is required to be opened in order to investigate the problems of the iron steel industry and determine the measures needed to be taken.
